ULTRASOUND APPEARANCE ENCODING RULE (MANDATORY WHEN SOURCE SUPPORTS)

If the provided Core/RadPrimer/STATdx source explicitly describes ultrasound appearance
(echogenicity, echotexture, posterior acoustic behavior, Doppler vascularity, compressibility, cystic-versus-solid morphology, source-stated sonographic signs/artifacts, or ultrasound-specific discriminator features):

You MUST generate at least ONE dedicated High-Yield card encoding that ultrasound appearance.

(If Sectioned Build mode is active, this requirement is QUEUED and must be emitted only in SECTION G.)

Rules:
- Ultrasound details may be included ONLY if explicitly stated in the source.
- Include the sonographic feature exactly as written when possible.
- Include Doppler, posterior acoustic behavior, compressibility, mobility, wall layering, maneuver findings, or source-named artifacts/signs ONLY if explicitly stated.
- Do NOT infer Doppler behavior unless source-supported.
- Do NOT infer posterior acoustic enhancement or shadowing unless source-supported.
- Do NOT infer compressibility unless source-supported.
- Do NOT infer wall stratification unless source-supported.
- Do NOT infer mobility, sludge/debris behavior, twinkle, ring-down, comet-tail, reverberation, or other ultrasound-specific artifact/sign behavior unless source-supported.
- Do NOT generalize beyond the specific source statement.
- Keep the card tight: 2-6 short lines max.

Recommended format:

High_Yield_Q:
"For [ENTITY], what sonographic appearance is expected (as stated in source)?"

High_Yield_A:
- Sonographic feature: [source-stated appearance]
- Doppler / acoustic / compressibility feature: [only if explicitly stated]
- Exam pivot: [one line: why this helps recognize or discriminate]

If the ultrasound description is too long:
- Split into multiple High-Yield cards ONLY if each card is its own distinct exam pivot.
- Otherwise omit lower-value details.

NUCLEAR MEDICINE APPEARANCE ENCODING RULE (MANDATORY WHEN SOURCE SUPPORTS)

- Do NOT infer uptake, photopenia, avidity, washout, or physiologic distribution unless source-supported.
- Do NOT generalize one tracer's behavior to another tracer.
- Do NOT convert source silence into a negative finding.
- Keep the card tight: 2-6 short lines max.

- Otherwise omit lower-value details.

SECTIONED BUILD RULE (QUALITY CONTROL)

SECTIONED BUILD OVERRIDE (QUEUE RULE â€” CRITICAL)

When Mode 1 Sectioned Review Output is active:

â€¢ Any rule elsewhere that says â€œMUST generateâ€ a card type
  (e.g., syndrome high-yield pairs, mechanism requirements, traps, etc.)
  MUST be treated as a QUEUED REQUIREMENT.

Meaning:
â€¢ The assistant MUST NOT emit cards outside the currently active section.
â€¢ Instead, it must queue the requirement and generate it only when the workflow reaches that section.

Examples:
â€¢ If a syndrome is introduced during SECTION A (PRIMARY UNKNOWN),
  the required syndrome High-Yield pair MUST be generated later in:
  SECTION G (High-Yield), not immediately.

â€¢ If a mechanism is implied/required during SECTION A,
  it must be generated later in:
  SECTION D (Mechanism), not immediately.

â€¢ If a Boards Trap is implied during any section,
  it MUST be generated later in:
  SECTION E (Boards Trap), not immediately.

â€¢ If epidemiology pivots exist in the source,
  they MUST be generated later in:
  SECTION F (Epidemiology), not immediately.

This rule supersedes all â€œgenerate immediatelyâ€ language elsewhere.
Cards must be generated and reviewed in discrete sections.
Do NOT generate â€œeverything in one shot.â€

Section order is STRICT:

SECTION A) PRIMARY UNKNOWN cases
SECTION B) Differential Drills (explicit request only)
SECTION C) Definition cards only (explicit request only)
SECTION D) Mechanism cards
SECTION E) Boards Trap cards
SECTION F) Epidemiology cards (High-Yield pivots only; if present in source)
SECTION G) High-Yield (non-epid, non-definition; includes diagnostic performance + outcomes when present)

Before outputting SECTION A, the assistant MUST already have completed:
â€¢ Core Validation Gate requirements
â€¢ Whole-Source Coverage Audit requirements
â€¢ Core Summary requirements when Pathway A is active

SECTION A CASE LEDGER (MANDATORY BEFORE PREVIEW)

Immediately before the SECTION A Structured Card Preview, the assistant MUST output a PRIMARY UNKNOWN case ledger that accounts for every user-provided intended SECTION A case.

The ledger MUST include:
â€¢ Total user-provided cases: X
â€¢ Eligible for PRIMARY UNKNOWN: Y
â€¢ EXCLUDED from PRIMARY UNKNOWN: Z
â€¢ PRIMARY UNKNOWN cards generated in SECTION A: Y

Then list:
â€¢ every excluded case with its one-line reason

Then explicitly confirm:
â€¢ "SECTION A cardinality check passed: eligible case count matches PRIMARY UNKNOWN count."

Section A case accounting rules:
â€¢ every user-provided intended SECTION A case must be assigned a case index
â€¢ every indexed case must be marked ELIGIBLE or EXCLUDED
â€¢ every EXCLUDED case must carry a one-line allowed reason
â€¢ every ELIGIBLE case must map one-to-one to exactly one PRIMARY UNKNOWN note
â€¢ Section A deduplication is forbidden
â€¢ no eligible case may be merged, substituted, or silently deferred

If the counts do NOT match:
â€¢ STOP
â€¢ Output: "SECTION A blocked - eligible case count does not match PRIMARY UNKNOWN count."
â€¢ Then list the missing or extra case(s) by case index.
â€¢ Do NOT preview SECTION A.

For each section:
â€¢ Output ONLY that sectionâ€™s Structured Card Preview.
â€¢ Do NOT output other sections.
â€¢ Wait for user approval (explicit â€œapprovedâ€ or â€œnext sectionâ€) before proceeding, UNLESS an autonomous batch mode override is present.
â€¢ If an autonomous batch mode override is present:
  â†’ section previews are internal validation checkpoints
  â†’ do NOT pause for user approval between sections
  â†’ do NOT output user-facing section-by-section previews unless a hard stop or major repair explanation must be shown

Structured Card Preview rules remain:
â€¢ Do NOT display full summary text anywhere in Review mode.
â€¢ For any RadPrimer/STATdx-origin card: display ONLY the note: â€œSummary field included.â€

-----------------------------------------------------

SECTION-LEVEL COVERAGE ENFORCEMENT (MANDATORY)

Before outputting each section preview, the assistant MUST verify that all content from the Whole-Source Coverage Audit relevant to that section has been considered.

For each section, the assistant must internally check:

â€¢ Which Core-derived items belong in this section
â€¢ Which article-derived items belong in this section
â€¢ Which non-overlapping high-yield items must be included to preserve mastery
â€¢ Which items are intentionally deferred to later sections under the queue rule

No section may be generated from digest-only understanding.
No section may be generated from Core Summary alone.
No section may be generated from summary field text alone.

Mode 2 â€” TSV Output (STRICT FORMAT):

â€¢ No header row.
â€¢ Tabs only.
â€¢ One note per line.
â€¢ Column count MUST be exactly:
  22 columns total = 21 fields (core_rad_notetype) + final Tags column.
â€¢ Clinical_Context can NEVER be empty.
â€¢ The final Tags column MUST remain present and MUST be blank / empty for every exported row.
â€¢ If any earlier rule conceptually references tag categories or ontology labels, those references MUST NOT populate the final TSV Tags column.
â€¢ The final TSV Tags column is an intentionally empty placeholder in this workflow.

TSV Structural Integrity Rule (CRITICAL)

â€¢ No literal newline characters (\n or \r) are allowed inside any field.
â€¢ Every note must occupy exactly one physical line in the TSV file.
â€¢ All internal formatting must use <br> instead of newline characters.
â€¢ Any violation invalidates the TSV.

Summary HTML Rule (TSV)
â€¢ The `summary` field must render as simple Anki-safe HTML, not raw markdown.
â€¢ Allowed summary HTML should remain limited to simple tags such as:
  <b> <br> <div> <ul> <ol> <li>
â€¢ The same article-level summary content must be reused identically across all cards from the same article/case bundle.
â€¢ The `summary` field must not contain literal emoji or other non-ASCII icon characters.
â€¢ Any summary icon anchor must be encoded as an HTML numeric character reference, such as `&#x1F50D;`, so TSV text stays ASCII-safe and does not turn into question marks during clipboard/console handling.

PREVIEW â†’ TSV IDENTITY LOCK (CRITICAL)

When the user says â€œTSVâ€ (or requests export/download):

2) The TSV MUST include:
   â€¢ Every HIGH-YIELD (including definition cards)
   â€¢ Every UNKNOWN
   â€¢ Every DIFFERENTIAL DRILL
   â€¢ Every MECHANISM

   â€¢ Add new cards
   â€¢ Remove cards
   â€¢ Merge/collapse cards
   â€¢ Substitute â€œexampleâ€ cards
   â€¢ Regenerate a simplified subset
   â€¢ Re-interpret architecture at export time

   except for minimal formatting adjustments needed to satisfy TSV rules
   (e.g., converting any accidental newlines to <br>).
5) Export order MUST match the preview order exactly
   (canonical order + within-section sequence),
   unless the user explicitly requests a different ordering.

ACTIVE PREVIEW SET DEFINITION (SECTIONED)

â€¢ In standard manual mode:
  â†’ The â€œactive preview setâ€ is the most recent USER-APPROVED section preview.
  â†’ TSV export is allowed ONLY for sections that have been explicitly approved.
  â†’ If a section is modified after approval:
    â†’ that section must be re-previewed and re-approved before TSV export.

â€¢ If an autonomous batch mode override is present:
  â†’ The â€œactive preview setâ€ is the most recent internally validated section or full-deck snapshot that passed all automatic checks.
  â†’ TSV export is allowed ONLY for sections that have passed automatic validation.
  â†’ If a section is modified after repair:
    â†’ that section must be internally re-validated before TSV export.

â€¢ Definition cards ONLY when the user explicitly requests definition cards

â€¢ Any intended Mechanism / Boards Trap / High-Yield (non-ontology)

Pre-TSV Validation Gate (MUST PASS BEFORE TSV):

A) Trigger Integrity (Architecture Safety)
â€¢ UNKNOWN cards: Image MUST be populated AND Question MUST be populated.
â€¢ If Image is populated but Question is empty â†’ it must NOT be treated as UNKNOWN.
â€¢ Any non-UNKNOWN card that includes Image MUST keep Question empty.
â€¢ Differential Drill exists ONLY when Differential_Q is populated.
â€¢ Differentials alone may be populated on a PRIMARY UNKNOWN note as back-side differential support.
â€¢ Mechanism exists ONLY when Mechanism is populated.
â€¢ Boards Trap exists ONLY when Boards_Trap is populated.
â€¢ High-Yield exists ONLY when High_Yield_Q is populated.
â€¢ No unintended extra card types may be created by accidental field population.

A1) SECTION A PRIMARY UNKNOWN CARDINALITY (MANDATORY)
â€¢ Every user-provided intended SECTION A case MUST appear in the case inventory.
â€¢ Every inventoried case MUST be marked ELIGIBLE or EXCLUDED.
â€¢ Every EXCLUDED case MUST include a one-line allowed reason.
â€¢ Every ELIGIBLE case MUST map one-to-one to exactly one PRIMARY UNKNOWN note.
â€¢ No two eligible cases may share one PRIMARY UNKNOWN note.
â€¢ No eligible case may be omitted, merged, substituted, or silently deferred.
â€¢ Eligible case count MUST equal PRIMARY UNKNOWN count.

If any of the above fails:
â€¢ STOP
â€¢ Output: "TSV export blocked - SECTION A case ledger / PRIMARY UNKNOWN cardinality rule violated."
â€¢ Then list the missing, extra, or improperly excluded case(s) by case index.
â€¢ Do NOT output TSV.

B) Source Coverage + Non-Fabrication
â€¢ All board-relevant Core content for the topic is represented (without trivial duplication), unless Core GAP pathway is invoked.
â€¢ All board-relevant RadPrimer/STATdx content for the topic is represented (when provided).
â€¢ All high-value non-overlapping article content needed for durable mastery is represented when article-based generation is being used.
â€¢ Whole-Source Coverage Audit requirements were completed before section generation.
â€¢ If Pathway A was used, Core Summary requirements were completed before SECTION A.
â€¢ No fabricated:
  imaging findings
  MRI signal/sequence behavior
  ultrasound behavior (echogenicity, echotexture, posterior acoustic behavior, Doppler behavior, compressibility, mobility, wall layering, maneuver findings, or source-named sonographic signs/artifacts)
  differentials (EXCEPT explicitly labeled Model-inferred differentials permitted under CONTROLLED INFERENCE)
  epidemiology
  thresholds / staging / management triggers
â€¢ If anything is inferred (rare): it MUST be explicitly labeled:
  â€œNot explicitly listed in Core/RadPrimer/STATdx; inferred based on imaging pattern.â€

C) Image/Captions â†’ Keyword Findings Integrity (UNKNOWN ONLY)
â€¢ For UNKNOWN cards:
  Most_Likely_Diagnosis must include caption-derived KEYWORD FINDINGS (3â€“8 phrases typical)
  Those phrases must be explicitly present in the verbatim captions
  No inference beyond caption text
â€¢ Image_Annotated order must match Image order (when present).

D) Differential Drill De-duplication (Pattern-Based; Discriminator-Driven; Explicit Request Only)

Core Principle:
Standalone drill generation is disabled unless the user explicitly requests separate differential drill cards.
When explicitly requested, drill generation is determined by Dominant Imaging Pattern and Primary Discriminator Logic â€” NOT by exact differential list equality.

STEP 1 â€” Identify Dominant Pattern
â€¢ For each potential Differential Drill, explicitly classify the dominant imaging pattern.
â€¢ The dominant pattern is defined by the primary discriminator that separates the leading entities.
â€¢ Examples:
  - Calcified Encasing Peritoneum Pattern
  - Smooth Diffuse Enhancing Peritoneum Pattern
  - Gas-Containing Ascites Pattern
  - Solid Polypoid Mass Pattern
  - Circumferential Stricture Pattern
  - Post-Operative Leak Pattern

STEP 2 â€” Deduplication Rule
â€¢ If two cases share the SAME dominant pattern AND the SAME primary discriminator logic:
  â†’ Generate ONLY ONE Differential Drill.
  â†’ All other cases must leave Differential_Q blank.
  â†’ Differential lists and discriminator reasoning may still appear on the associated UNKNOWN back in Differentials and Imaging_Differentiation.

â€¢ If two cases differ in dominant pattern OR require a different primary discriminator strategy:
  â†’ A new Differential Drill is ALLOWED.
  â†’ Overlap in differential lists does NOT prevent generation.

STEP 3 â€” Inflation Suppression
Do NOT generate a new drill if:
â€¢ The same mental separation strategy would be repeated.
â€¢ The same dominant discriminator is being trained.
â€¢ Only minor list variation exists without a new cognitive pivot.

STEP 4 â€” Justification Requirement
If generating a new drill in a topic where another drill already exists,
Imaging_Differentiation MUST begin with:
â€œDominant pattern differs from other cases because: [distinct discriminator explanation].â€

This enforces conscious pattern separation and prevents silent duplication.

Key Rule:
Pattern distinctiveness overrides list variation.
Exact differential-set comparison is NOT the deduplication mechanism.

E) Differential Drill Structural Enforcement (CRITICAL)
Applies to every note where Differential_Q is populated:

Required Field Presence + Isolation
â€¢ Differential_Q MUST be populated.
â€¢ Imaging_Differentiation MUST be populated.
â€¢ Question MUST be blank.
â€¢ Mechanism_Q / Mechanism MUST be blank.
â€¢ Boards_Trap_Q / Boards_Trap MUST be blank.
â€¢ High_Yield_Q / High_Yield_A MUST be blank.

Mode Determination (MANDATORY)
â€¢ If a drill originates from an existing UNKNOWN case:
  â†’ This Differential Drill MUST be MODE 1 (case-bundled).
  â†’ Standalone mode is NOT allowed.
â€¢ If no such UNKNOWN exists:
  â†’ MODE 2 may be used (rare), but must satisfy Mode 2 rules below.

MODE 1 (Case-Bundled) Requirements
â€¢ Clinical_Context MUST preserve the same blinded clinical wording as the associated UNKNOWN card, but MUST be unique at the note level to prevent duplicate-note conflicts.
â€¢ A short deterministic suffix (e.g., [DD1]) is allowed and expected for case-bundled Differential Drills.
â€¢ Image MUST be populated and MUST match the associated UNKNOWN Image exactly.
â€¢ If the associated UNKNOWN card contains Image_Annotated (non-empty):
  â†’ The Differential Drill MUST include the exact same Image_Annotated block
    (same stackItem blocks + verbatim captions).
  â†’ Missing Image_Annotated in this scenario = structural failure.
â€¢ Entity_Label is RECOMMENDED and must contain diagnosis only (no descriptors/findings/reasoning).
â€¢ Most_Likely_Diagnosis must be blank OR diagnosis-only (single line). No keyword findings or reasoning.

MODE 2 (Standalone) Requirements (RARE)

â€¢ Entity_Label is REQUIRED (diagnosis-only).
â€¢ Image is OPTIONAL.
â€¢ Most_Likely_Diagnosis must be blank OR diagnosis-only (single line).

Front-Side Blinding Audit (Differential Drill)

  Clinical_Context

  Image stack (if present)
â€¢ Front must NOT contain:
  diagnosis names (except neutral Entity_Label if template prints it)
  named differentials
  caption-derived keyword findings
  named signs
  management clues

=== DIFFERENTIAL DIAGNOSIS ===
- [RadPrimer] Common: Focal nodular hyperplasia; hepatic cavernous hemangioma.
- [RadPrimer] Less common: Hepatocellular carcinoma; hepatic adenoma; hepatic metastases; fibrolamellar hepatocellular carcinoma; intrahepatic/peripheral cholangiocarcinoma; epithelioid hemangioendothelioma.
- [RadPrimer] Rare but important: Nodular regenerative hyperplasia, especially large regenerative nodules in Budd-Chiari syndrome.

=== HIGH-YIELD DISCRIMINATORS ===
- [Both] Start with scar size, scar calcification, enhancement pattern of the mass/scar, multiplicity, and associated findings such as vascular invasion, biliary obstruction, capsular retraction, or Budd-Chiari morphology.
- [Both] FNH and cholangiocarcinoma are usually solitary, fibrolamellar carcinoma is almost always solitary, and hemangiomas, metastases, epithelioid hemangioendothelioma, HCC, and nodular regenerative hyperplasia may be multiple depending on clinical setting.
- [Both] Calcified scar strongly favors fibrolamellar HCC or giant hemangioma. Calcification is uncommon/rare in FNH, conventional HCC, adenoma, cholangiocarcinoma, and most metastases, but mucinous metastases such as colon/ovarian cancer can calcify.
- [Both] A true scar should be separated from necrosis, hemorrhage, fibrosis, fat, or treated tumor. Adenoma and metastases often mimic scar because of hemorrhage, necrosis, fat, or fibrosis rather than a true central scar.

=== DIAGNOSIS-SPECIFIC SYNTHESIS ===
- [Both] Focal nodular hyperplasia: Larger lesions often have a small central scar and thin radiating septa. The mass enhances homogeneously in the arterial phase and becomes isoattenuating/isointense to liver on later phases. The scar is bright on T2, may show delayed enhancement with CT or intravascular MR agents, and does not enhance/retain gadoxetate on delayed hepatobiliary imaging. Use STATdx images 1-3 as the primary FNH teaching cluster.
- [Both] Hepatic cavernous hemangioma: Large or giant hemangiomas may have a fibrotic nonenhancing central scar that may calcify, while nonscar tissue shows peripheral nodular discontinuous enhancement with progressive fill-in. Very bright T2 signal supports hemangioma. Use STATdx images 4-6 as the primary hemangioma cluster.
- [Both] Conventional hepatocellular carcinoma: Large tumors, especially in a noncirrhotic liver, can resemble fibrolamellar HCC because they are heterogeneous, hypervascular, and may have washout with central necrosis or scar. Scar calcification is rare in conventional HCC. Vascular invasion and metastatic disease support HCC. Use STATdx images 7-8 for mosaic enhancement, necrosis/scar, and portal vein tumor invasion.
- [Both] Hepatic adenoma: Low-density or low-signal regions usually represent fat, necrosis, or old hemorrhage rather than a true scar. Chemical-shift MRI can show signal drop from fat. Calcification is uncommon. Eovist-enhanced MRI is most useful for distinction from FNH. Use STATdx images 9-10 as adenoma-pattern reinforcement for hemorrhage/necrosis/fibrosis and nonenhancing central abnormality.
- [Both] Hepatic metastases: Target appearance and central low density usually reflect necrosis or fibrosis rather than a true scar. Fibrous stroma may be prominent after treatment or in some primaries. Calcification is uncommon overall but can be seen with mucinous metastases such as colon or ovarian cancer. Use STATdx images 11-12 together as the colon-cancer metastasis cluster.
- [Both] Fibrolamellar HCC: Classically a large heterogeneous mass in a young patient, often with a large central scar that is calcified in more than half of cases. Aggressive features such as local vascular/biliary invasion and metastases are common at presentation. Use STATdx images 13-15 as the fibrolamellar cluster, including nodal disease and calcified scar.
- [Both] Intrahepatic/peripheral cholangiocarcinoma: Focal necrosis or fibrosis can mimic scar. Extensive fibrous stroma produces progressive delayed enhancement. Capsular retraction, hepatic volume loss, biliary obstruction, and portal venous obstruction are key supporting clues. Use STATdx images 16-17 as the CT/MR cholangiocarcinoma cluster.
- [Both] Epithelioid hemangioendothelioma: Usually multiple peripheral nodules that may coalesce. Target or lollipop appearance and capsular retraction are important clues. Use STATdx images 18-19 for target/capsular retraction and T2 central necrosis or scar.
- [Both] Nodular regenerative hyperplasia / large regenerative nodules: Multiacinar large regenerative nodules can have central scars, are usually multiple and less than 4 cm, and are classically associated with Budd-Chiari syndrome. Hepatobiliary agent retention supports functional hepatocytes with deficient biliary ducts. Use STATdx images 20-24 as the Budd-Chiari/NRH imaging-pathology cluster, including trichrome confirmation.

=== SELECTED PRIMARY IMAGE SET ===
- STATdx image 1: Axial arterial-phase CECT shows a homogeneously and brightly enhancing mass <img src="arrow_WS.png"> with a central scar <img src="arrow_BC.png">. The mass became isodense with liver on portal venous phase. Used for: focalNodularHyperplasia, arterialHyperenhancement, centralScar, recognitionReinforcement, axialArterialCECT.
- STATdx image 2: Axial 20-minute delayed T1 MR shows a large mass <img src="arrow_WS.png"> that retains contrast more than underlying liver. The central scar <img src="arrow_WC.png"> does not take up or retain gadoxetate. Used for: focalNodularHyperplasia, hepatobiliaryContrast, gadoxetateScarNonretention, recognitionReinforcement, delayedT1MR.
- STATdx image 3: Axial T1 C+ FS MR shows a brightly and homogeneously enhancing mass with a central scar <img src="arrow_BC.png">; this is classic FNH. Used for: focalNodularHyperplasia, classicHomogeneousEnhancement, centralScar, STATdxSupplementalImage, recognitionReinforcement, axialT1PostContrastMR.
- STATdx image 4: Axial CECT shows a large mass with peripheral nodular enhancement <img src="arrow_WS.png"> isodense to vessels. A central scar <img src="arrow_WO.png">, with a small focus of calcification, did not fill in on delayed imaging. These are typical features of giant hemangiomas. Used for: giantHemangioma, peripheralNodularEnhancement, calcifiedCentralScar, recognitionReinforcement, axialCECT.
- STATdx image 5: Axial T2 MR shows a large, hyperintense mass with a central scar <img src="arrow_BC.png"> that is very bright, typical of a large hemangioma with progressive nodular enhancement on arterial <img src="arrow_WS.png">, venous <img src="arrow_WO.png">, and delayed <img src="arrow_BS.png"> phases. Used for: giantHemangioma, veryBrightT2Scar, multiphasicProgressiveEnhancement, atomicMultipanelCluster, recognitionReinforcement, axialT2MR.
- STATdx image 6: Axial T2 MR shows a large mass <img src="arrow_WS.png"> that is very bright and well defined. A central scar <img src="arrow_BC.png"> is even more intense, typical of large cavernous hemangioma. Used for: cavernousHemangioma, veryBrightT2Mass, STATdxSupplementalImage, recognitionReinforcement, axialT2MR.
- STATdx image 7: Axial arterial-phase CECT in an older woman shows a large, heterogeneous, hypervascular mass <img src="arrow_WS.png"> with foci of necrosis, giving it a mottled or mosaic appearance. The age of the patient and absence of calcification favored conventional hepatocellular carcinoma (HCC), as opposed to fibrolamellar HCC, confirmed on biopsy. Used for: conventionalHCC, mosaicMottledEnhancement, necrosis, olderPatient, recognitionReinforcement, axialArterialCECT.
- STATdx image 8: Axial CECT shows a large mass with an eccentric scar <img src="arrow_WC.png"> or necrotic area. Tumor invasion of portal vein <img src="arrow_WS.png"> helps to identify this as HCC. Used for: hepatocellularCarcinoma, eccentricScarOrNecrosis, portalVeinTumorInvasion, recognitionReinforcement, axialCECT.
- STATdx image 9: Axial NECT shows a heterogeneous mass in the left lobe. At resection, the focus of high attenuation <img src="arrow_WO.png"> was hemorrhage, and the lower density foci <img src="arrow_WC.png"> were necrosis and fibrosis. Used for: hepaticAdenomaPattern, hemorrhageNecrosisFibrosis, leftLobeMass, recognitionReinforcement, axialNECT.
- STATdx image 10: Axial venous-phase T1 C+ MR shows a large, enhancing mass <img src="arrow_WS.png"> with a central nonenhancing scar <img src="arrow_WC.png">. Used for: hepaticAdenomaPattern, enhancingMassWithNonenhancingScar, MRVariant, recognitionReinforcement, venousPhaseT1PostContrastMR.
- STATdx image 11: Axial NECT in this older patient with colon cancer shows shows multiple lesions with faint central calcification <img src="arrow_WS.png">. Used for: mucinousColonMetastases, centralCalcification, noncontrastDetection, samePatientCluster, recognitionReinforcement, axialNECT.
- STATdx image 12: Axial portal venous-phase CECT in this older patient with colon cancer shows multiple metastases <img src="arrow_CS.png"> with central, low-density necrosis or fibrosis. The faint calcification is more difficult to recognize on contrast-enhanced imaging. Used for: mucinousColonMetastases, centralNecrosisOrFibrosis, contrastPhaseComparison, samePatientCluster, recognitionReinforcement, portalVenousCECT.
- STATdx image 13: Axial CECT in a 22-year-old man shows a heterogeneous, hypervascular mass with a large, calcified central scar <img src="arrow_WC.png">. Note the similar enhancement of tumor in a cardiophrenic node <img src="arrow_WS.png">. Used for: fibrolamellarHCC, calcifiedCentralScar, hypervascularTumor, cardiophrenicNodeMetastasis, recognitionReinforcement, axialCECT.
- STATdx image 14: Axial T1 C+ FS MR in a young woman with a large, heterogeneously enhancing left hepatic lobe fibrolamellar HCC shows a central necrotic scar <img src="arrow_WS.png">. Used for: fibrolamellarHCC, necroticCentralScar, youngWoman, MRVariant, recognitionReinforcement, T1PostContrastFSMR.
- STATdx image 15: Axial CECT shows a large mass that fills the left lobe with low density <img src="arrow_BC.png"> and calcified <img src="arrow_BS.png"> portions of the scar. Used for: fibrolamellarHCC, calcifiedScar, largeLeftLobeMass, STATdxSupplementalImage, recognitionReinforcement, axialCECT.
- STATdx image 16: Axial CECT shows a large hepatic mass with foci of scar or necrosis <img src="arrow_WC.png">. Capsular retraction and occlusion of the portal vein <img src="arrow_WS.png"> and bile duct <img src="arrow_BS.png"> help confirm cholangiocarcinoma. Used for: cholangiocarcinoma, capsularRetraction, portalVeinOcclusion, bileDuctOcclusion, recognitionReinforcement, axialCECT.
- STATdx image 17: Axial T1 C+ FS MR shows a large, hypointense mass <img src="arrow_WS.png"> with progressive enhancement on arterial <img src="arrow_WO.png">, portal venous <img src="arrow_WC.png">, and delayed <img src="arrow_BS.png"> phases due to fibrotic changes and a nonenhancing central scar <img src="arrow_CS.png">. Used for: cholangiocarcinoma, progressiveFibroticEnhancement, nonenhancingCentralScar, atomicMultipanelCluster, recognitionReinforcement, T1PostContrastFSMR.
- STATdx image 18: Axial CECT in a young woman shows multiple peripheral hypovascular lesions with a target appearance. The subcapsular lesions are associated with retraction of the overlying liver capsule <img src="arrow_WS.png">. Used for: epithelioidHemangioendothelioma, targetAppearance, subcapsularCapsularRetraction, recognitionReinforcement, axialCECT.
- STATdx image 19: Axial T2 FS MR in a young woman shows multiple peripheral and confluent masses <img src="arrow_WS.png">, many with hyperintense central necrosis or scar <img src="arrow_WC.png">. These are typical features of hepatic epithelial hemangioendothelioma. Used for: epithelioidHemangioendothelioma, multifocalPeripheralConfluentMasses, T2CentralNecrosisOrScar, recognitionReinforcement, T2FSMR.
- STATdx image 20: Axial arterial-phase T1 C+ MR in a hypercoagulable patient shows multiple hypervascular foci <img src="arrow_WS.png">. Some of the lesions seem to have a hypointense rim, while others have a hypointense central scar <img src="arrow_CS.png">. Used for: nodularRegenerativeHyperplasia, BuddChiari, hypervascularNodules, centralScar, samePatientCluster, recognitionReinforcement, arterialT1PostContrastMR.
- STATdx image 21: Axial T1 C+ MR in a hypercoagulable patient 2 hours after IV administration of gadobenate dimeglumine shows persistent uptake and retention of the agent within the nodules <img src="arrow_BS.png">, indicating functional hepatocytes and deficient biliary ducts within the lesions. Used for: nodularRegenerativeHyperplasia, BuddChiari, hepatobiliaryAgentRetention, functionalHepatocytes, samePatientCluster, recognitionReinforcement, delayedT1PostContrastMR.
- STATdx image 22: Axial T1 C+ MR in a patient with Budd-Chiari syndrome shows multiple hypervascular nodules <img src="arrow_WS.png">, some of which have a central scar <img src="arrow_WC.png">. These multiple large, regenerative nodules (nodular regenerative hyperplasia) confirmed transplantation. Used for: nodularRegenerativeHyperplasia, BuddChiari, multipleLargeRegenerativeNodules, transplantConfirmation, recognitionReinforcement, T1PostContrastMR.
- STATdx image 23: Axial CECT in a patient with Budd-Chiari syndrome and nodular regenerative hyperplasia shows a central scar <img src="arrow_WS.png"> and doughnut-like enhancement. Transjugular intrahepatic portosystemic shunt (TIPS) is also seen <img src="arrow_WC.png">. Used for: nodularRegenerativeHyperplasia, BuddChiari, doughnutEnhancement, TIPSContext, recognitionReinforcement, axialCECT.
- STATdx image 24: In this patient with Budd-Chiari syndrome and multiple hypervascular nodules, trichrome stain shows green staining of the central scar <img src="arrow_WC.png"> and the capsule <img src="arrow_WS.png"> of the nodule, typical features of the macroscopic form of nodular regenerative hyperplasia or large regenerative nodules. Used for: nodularRegenerativeHyperplasia, BuddChiari, histologyCorrelation, trichromeCentralScar, STATdxSupplementalImage, recognitionReinforcement.

=== CASE CLUSTER NOTES ===
- No source case cluster was intentionally split.
- STATdx image 1, STATdx image 2, STATdx image 3: FNH cluster spanning arterial CT/MR enhancement and hepatobiliary delayed behavior of the scar.
- STATdx image 4, STATdx image 5, STATdx image 6: Hemangioma cluster showing peripheral nodular enhancement, very bright T2 signal, and fibrotic/calcified scar behavior.
- STATdx image 11, STATdx image 12: Same older colon cancer patient: noncontrast calcification and portal venous necrotic/fibrotic metastases should stay together.
- STATdx image 13, STATdx image 14, STATdx image 15: Fibrolamellar HCC cluster demonstrating calcified scar, MR necrotic scar, left-lobe mass, and nodal/metastatic context.
- STATdx image 16, STATdx image 17: Cholangiocarcinoma cluster pairs obstructive/capsular retraction CT findings with progressive fibrotic MR enhancement.
- STATdx image 18, STATdx image 19: Epithelioid hemangioendothelioma CT/MR cluster showing peripheral target lesions and T2 central necrosis/scar.
- STATdx image 20, STATdx image 21, STATdx image 22, STATdx image 23, STATdx image 24: Budd-Chiari/nodular regenerative hyperplasia cluster including hepatobiliary agent retention, TIPS context, transplant confirmation, and histology correlation.
- RadPrimer images 1-20 were archived only as exact duplicate renderings because the equivalent STATdx source-image sequence was selected as the complete curated primary set.
